<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016NtCCqJ2QZVncyZQT2GZwc`</summary>

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_11/projects/phase-gated-compound-sparse/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — Phase-Gated Compound Sparsity\\n\\n> 最后更新: 2026-05-14 | 维护者: Director\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nCompound sparsity（attention head pruning + KV cache eviction）的 severity 不是固定常数——它是 eviction strategy 和 reasoning phase 交互的涌现属性。Importance-based eviction 在 low-sparsity reasoning phase 产生 11-42× severity amplification（problem-level bootstrap），而 random eviction 保持 ~1× 平坦。这一发现挑战了 compound degradation 均匀分布的隐含假设。\\n\\n**⚠️ 数字基准**：本文件中的具体数字（gradient magnitude, crossover values）基于 fp16 problem-level bootstrap 结果。bf16 重跑完成后必须统一更新。segment-level 估计（12-75×）已弃用，仅在被明确标注时作为参考。\\n\\n## Narrative Arc\\n\\nCompound sparsity 是高效 LLM 推理的实用部署需求，但 prior work（SparK, Ada-KV, SLiM）将 compound interaction 视为均匀超可加。我们发现 severity 是 strategy × phase 交互的涌现属性：importance eviction 在 distributed-attention phase 剧烈放大 compound degradation（problem-level gradient 11-42×），而 random eviction 在所有 phase 保持平坦（~1×）。我们通过 entropy-discrimination collapse 机制解释这一现象：head pruning → attention 重分布 → importance score 的判别力崩塌 → importance eviction 退化为有偏伪随机。这一发现为 phase-aware sparsity allocation 提供了动机。\\n\\n**Framing 硬约束（D015）**：\\n- MUST: \\\"emergent phase-strategy interaction\\\"\\n- MUST NOT: \\\"importance metric limitation / importance is broken\\\"\\n- MUST NOT: claim \\\"importance masking produces super-additive interaction\\\" without qualifier — random masking also produces positive II (~0.9-1.3×). The distinguishing signal is **phase-dependent gradient** (importance: 0.49→4.95→25.88 vs random: ~1× flat across phases), not the existence of super-additivity itself (D023)\\n\\n## Core Claims\\n\\n| ID | Claim | Status | Key Evidence |\\n|----|-------|--------|-------------|\\n| C1 | Compound sparsity severity is strategy-dependent | confirmed | importance gradient 11-42× (problem-level) vs random ~1×. 待 bf16 数据更新 |\\n| C2 | Severity is phase-modulated (amplified in low-sparsity phase) | confirmed | low phase >> high phase at all 4 sparsity levels. Problem-level: 11.53→11.05→35.72→41.92（s015→s020 非严格单调，bf16 数据可能改变趋势。论文措辞用 \\\"increases with sparsity\\\" 而非 \\\"monotonically increases\\\"） |\\n| C3 | Phase-strategy crossover exists | confirmed | s=0.25: importance high_phase=0.584 < random=3.002, importance low_phase=30.903 > random=2.915 |\\n| C4 | Entropy-discrimination collapse explains the mechanism | partially_confirmed | Conceptual argument solid; empirical attention entropy data not yet collected |\\n| C5 | Finding generalizes across models (same arch, different training) | pending | Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct experiments in progress. Note: DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B 和 Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct 共享 Qwen2ForCausalLM 架构，区别在训练方式（distillation vs instruction tuning）。是 cross-model 而非 cross-architecture replication。 |\\n| C6 | Severity gradient is sub-exponential | confirmed | Growth rate decreasing: 2.24×→1.84×→1.42× |\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n### RC1: Generalizability — 7B models only, single benchmark\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"Results on two 7B models and MATH-500 only. How do we know this generalizes to larger models or other reasoning tasks?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Cross-model replication (DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B + Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) 控制架构变量、隔离训练方式影响（distillation vs instruction tuning）。注意：两模型共享 Qwen2ForCausalLM 架构，不能 claim 跨架构泛化。MATH-500 是 standard benchmark。Limitations 必须诚实声明：(1) 同架构不同训练，不是跨架构；(2) 7B scale only；(3) 单 benchmark。机制解释 (entropy-discrimination collapse) 原则上 architecture-agnostic，但未实证验证。\\n- **实验支撑**: C5 (Qwen cross-model). 若需加强可扩展到不同架构（如 LLaMA/Mistral），但 11 天 deadline 内不现实\\n\\n### RC2: Mechanism — entropy-discrimination collapse 缺乏直接测量\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"The mechanism is conceptual. Where is the empirical evidence for entropy increase and discrimination collapse?\\\"\\n- **应对**: 如果有 GPU 预算，可以直接测量 pruned vs unpruned 的 attention entropy 和 importance score 分布。目前 C4 status = partially_confirmed，需要决定是否投入 GPU 时间做 direct measurement。\\n- **实验支撑**: 可选 ablation：测量 attention entropy pre/post pruning × phase\\n\\n### RC3: Practical impact — 没有 throughput 数据\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"No wall-clock or throughput measurements. Is the severity amplification reflected in actual inference speed?\\\"\\n- **应对**: 本文定位是 empirical finding paper（发现 + 机制解释），不是 method paper。Throughput 不是 claim 的一部分。Discussion 中明确 phase-gated controller 作为 future direction。\\n- **实验支撑**: 不需要额外实验\\n\\n### RC4: Statistical rigor — bootstrap 是否足够\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"Per-problem bootstrap with 50 problems — sufficient sample size?\\\"\\n- **应对**: **⚠️ fp16 profiling 实际使用 B=1000（非论文中误写的 10000），p 值精度上限为 p < 0.001。bf16 重跑已将 n_bootstrap 设为 10000 以支撑更强的 p 值声明。** fp16 下实际配对数为 16-22/50（NaN 过滤后），bf16 预期恢复到 ~45-50 配对。bf16 数据到手后需重新验证统计效力。可在 appendix 补充 per-problem 分布图。**注意**：random baseline 的 high_sparsity II skewness=-4.83, mid_sparsity=+5.14（极端偏斜），bootstrap mean CI 不稳定。permutation test 必须使用 winsorized mean 或 median 作为 robust statistic，论文中同时报告 median 作为稳健性检查。\\n- **实验支撑**: fp16 bootstrap (B=1000) 已完成；bf16 bootstrap (B=10000) 待重做（plan_008）\\n\\n### RC5: Novelty — prior work 是否已覆盖 phase-dependent sparsity\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"ThinKV and phase-aware methods already consider phase. What's new?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Prior work 在单轴上分析 phase（ThinKV: phase-aware eviction）或 compound（SparK: joint compression）。没有工作同时分析 compound × phase × strategy 的三维交互。Crossover 现象（同一 strategy 在不同 phase 方向反转）是全新发现。\\n- **实验支撑**: C3 (crossover), Related Work §2 的 gap 分析\\n\\n## Paper Positioning\\n\\n**类型**: Empirical finding paper（非 method paper）\\n**核心贡献**:\\n1. 发现 compound sparsity severity 的 strategy × phase 交互\\n2. 量化 severity gradient（problem-level 11-42×, 待 bf16 更新）和 crossover 现象\\n3. 提出 entropy-discrimination collapse 机制解释\\n4. Cross-model replication 验证泛化性\\n\\n**投稿目标**: EMNLP 2026 ARR, deadline 2026-05-25\\n\", \"total_chars\": 5501, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

The distinguishing signal is **phase-dependent gradient** (importance: 0.49→4.95→25.88 vs random: ~1× flat across phases), not the existence of super-additivity itself (D023)\\\\n\\\\n## Core Claims\\\\n\\\\n| ID | Claim | Status | Key Evidence |\\\\n|----|-------|--------|-------------|\\\\n| C1 | Compound sparsity severity is strategy-dependent | confirmed | importance gradient 11-42× (problem-level) vs random ~1×. 待 bf16 数据更新 |\\\\n| C2 | Severity is phase-modulated (amplified in low-sparsity phase) | confirmed | low phase >> high phase at all 4 sparsity levels. Problem-level: 11.53→11.05→35.72→41.92（s015→s020 非严格单调，bf16 数据可能改变趋势。论文措辞用 \\\\\\\"increases with sparsity\\\\\\\" 而非 \\\\\\\"monotonically increases\\\\\\\"） |\\\\n| C3 | Phase-strategy crossover exists | confirmed | s=0.25: importance high_phase=0.584 < random=3.002, importance low_phase=30.903 > random=2.915 |\\\\n| C4 | Entropy-discrimination collapse explains the mechanism | partially_confirmed | Conceptual argument solid; empirical attention entropy data not yet collected |\\\\n| C5 | Finding generalizes across models (same arch, different training) | pending | Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct experiments in progress. Note: DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B 和 Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct 共享 Qwen2ForCausalLM 架构，区别在训练方式（distillation vs instruction tuning）。是 cross-model 而非 cross-architecture replication。 |\\\\n| C6 | Severity gradient is sub-exponential | confirmed | Growth rate decreasing: 2.24×→1.84×→1.42× |\\\\n\\\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\\\n\\\\n### RC1: Generalizability — 7B models only, single benchmark\\\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\\\\\"Results on two 7B models and MATH-500 only. Where is the empirical evidence for entropy increase and discrimination collapse?\\\\\\\"\\\\n- **应对**: 如果有 GPU 预算，可以直接测量 pruned vs unpruned 的 attention entropy 和 importance score 分布。目前 C4 status = partially_confirmed，需要决定是否投入 GPU 时间做 direct measurement。\\\\n- **实验支撑**: 可选 ablation：测量 attention entropy pre/post pruning × phase\\\\n\\\\n### RC3: Practical impact — 没有 throughput 数据\\\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\\\\\"No wall-clock or throughput measurements. I found comprehensive information about SnapKV from the 2024 paper by Yuhong Li. Here's what I found:

## Overview

SnapKV is an innovative and fine-tuning-free approach that efficiently minimizes KV cache size while still delivering comparable performance in real-world applications.

## Key Insight: Observation Window and Attention Patterns

Each attention head in the model consistently focuses on specific prompt attention features during generation, and this robust pattern can be obtained from an 'observation' window located at the end of the prompts.

## Compression Mechanism

SnapKV automatically compresses KV caches by selecting clustered important KV positions for each attention head. More specifically, the method concatenates the selected attention features with all features within the observation window, which encompasses all features containing the necessary prompt information.

## Attention Scoring and Parameters

The method uses attention features from the prompt for answer generation, with a maximum pooling kernel size of 5 and an observation window size of 16, both of which are hyperparameters that can be customized.

## Performance Results

SnapKV achieves a consistent decoding speed with a 3.6x increase in generation speed and an 8.2x enhancement in memory efficiency compared to the baseline when processing inputs of 16K tokens. Additionally, SnapKV demonstrates potential to precisely manage small details on extremely long input contexts with a 380x compression ratio.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

More specifically, the method concatenates the selected attention features with all features within the observation window, which encompasses all features containing the necessary prompt information.\n\n## Attention Scoring and Parameters\n\nThe method uses attention features from the prompt for answer generation, with a maximum pooling kernel size of 5 and an observation window size of 16, both of which are hyperparameters that can be customized.\n\n## Performance Results\n\nSnapKV achieves a consistent decoding speed with a 3.6x increase in generation speed and an 8.2x enhancement in memory efficiency compared to the baseline when processing inputs of 16K tokens. Additionally, SnapKV demonstrates potential to precisely manage small details on extremely long input contexts with a 380x compression ratio."

I found comprehensive information about the NACL KV cache eviction policy paper from 2024. Here's what I found:

## Overview

NACL is titled "A General and Effective KV Cache Eviction Framework for LLM at Inference Time" and was presented at the 62nd Annual Meeting of the Association for Computational Linguistics in Bangkok, Thailand by the Association for Computational Linguistics.

## Key Approach

NACL is a general framework for long-context KV cache eviction that achieves more optimal and efficient eviction in a single operation during the encoding phase. NACL introduces a hybrid KV cache eviction policy combining PROXY-TOKENS EVICTION and RANDOM EVICTION, where PROXY-TOKENS EVICTION identifies a subset of proxy tokens within the input that accurately estimates token importance.

## Results

The method significantly improves the performance on short- and long-text tasks by 80% and 76% respectively, reducing KV Cache by up to 5× with over 95% performance maintenance.

## Problem Addressed

The extensive memory consumption of KV Cache involving long-context modeling is cost-prohibitive, and most existing works rely on the biased local statistics of accumulated attention scores.

Code is available at https://github.com/PaddlePaddle/Research/tree/master/NLP/ACL2024-NACL.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

Bootstrap CI (B=10000) confirms significance at all 4 sparsity levels (p<0.0001).\\n        Cross-model replication on Qwen2.5-7B validates generalizability. We explain this via an\\n        entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism: head pruning distributes surviving attention,\\n        destroying importance score discriminability specifically in distributed-attention phases.\\n      length: 250 words\\n\\n    - id: introduction\\n      title: \\\"1. Introduction\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Motivate compound sparsity as practical deployment need. State gap: prior work (SparK, Ada-KV)\\n        treats compound interaction as uniformly super-additive. Our finding: the severity is an\\n        emergent property of the interaction between eviction strategy and reasoning phase.\\n        Preview crossover phenomenon and mechanism. State contributions.\\n      length: 1 page\\n      key_claims:\\n        - Compound sparsity severity is not uniform — it is strategy-dependent and phase-modulated\\n        - Importance-based eviction amplifies compound interaction 12-75× in low-sparsity phases\\n        - Entropy-discrimination collapse explains the phase-strategy interaction\\n\\n    - id: related_work\\n      title: \\\"2. Related Work\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Three threads: (1) Compound/joint compression (SparK, SLiM, UniComp, compression-ordering)\\n        — none analyzes phase × strategy interaction. (2) KV cache eviction (Ada-KV, DefensiveKV,\\n        SnapKV, NACL) — importance fragility noted but not phase-conditioned. (3) Phase-aware\\n        inference (ThinKV, PagedEviction, reasoning dynamics) — single-axis, no compound interaction.\\n      length: 0.75 page\\n      key_refs:\\n        - spark-aaai2026\\n        - thinkv-iclr2026\\n        - defensivekv-iclr2026\\n        - slim-icml2025\\n        - phase-transition-compression-2026\\n        - decision-repr-transition-2026\\n        - retentivekv-2026\\n        - greedy-bias-kvcache-2026\\n\\n    - id: background\\n      title: \\\"3. Define Interaction\\n        Index (II) measuring super/sub-additivity. Define phase segmentation via ThinKV-style\\n        token-ratio method. Formalize the research question: is II constant across phases and strategies?\\n      length: 1 page\\n      subsections:\\n        - \\\"3.1 Compound Sparsity Formulation\\\"\\n        - \\\"3.2 Interaction Index\\\"\\n        - \\\"3.3 Phase Segmentation\\\"\\n\\n    - id: methodology\\n      title: \\\"4. Results\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Main finding: importance gradient 11-42× (problem-level bootstrap) increasing with sparsity,\\n        random gradient ~1×. Analysis: Entropy-Discrimination Collapse\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Mechanistic explanation: head pruning → surviving heads attend more uniformly → importance\\n        score entropy increases → discrimination between important/unimportant KV entries collapses →\\n        importance-based eviction degrades to biased pseudo-random in distributed-attention phases.\\n        This is phase-dependent because reasoning phases have more distributed attention.\\n      length: 1 page\\n\\n    - id: discussion\\n      title: \\\"7. Discussion\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Implications for compound sparsity deployment: uniform budgets are unsafe.\\n        Phase-gated controllers as solution direction. Bootstrap CI (B=10000) confirms significance at all 4 sparsity levels (p<0.0001).\\\\n        Cross-model replication on Qwen2.5-7B validates generalizability. Introduction\\\\\\\"\\\\n      summary: >\\\\n        Motivate compound sparsity as practical deployment need.       "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_11/projects/phase-gated-compound-sparse/writing_plan.yaml\", \"content\": \"## Writing Plan — Phase-Gated Compound Sparsity\\n## 最后更新: 2026-05-14\\n\\n# ============================================================\\n# 投稿目标\\n# ============================================================\\ntarget:\\n  venue: EMNLP 2026 ARR\\n  deadline: 2026-05-25\\n  days_remaining: 11\\n  format: long paper (8 pages + references + appendix)\\n  anonymization: ARR double-blind\\n\\n# ============================================================\\n# Paper Outline\\n# ============================================================\\noutline:\\n  title_candidates:\\n    - \\\"Phase-Modulated Compound Sparsity: How Eviction Strategy and Reasoning Phase Interact in Sparse LLM Inference\\\"\\n    - \\\"Emergent Phase-Strategy Interaction in Compound Sparse LLM Inference\\\"\\n    - \\\"When Compound Sparsity Bites Back: Phase-Dependent Severity Amplification in Importance-Based Eviction\\\"\\n\\n  sections:\\n    - id: abstract\\n      title: Abstract\\n      summary: >\\n        Compound sparsity (head pruning + KV cache eviction) is assumed to degrade uniformly.\\n        We show the interaction is strategy-dependent and phase-modulated: importance-based eviction\\n        exhibits 12-75× severity amplification in low-sparsity reasoning phases, while random eviction\\n        stays flat (~1×). Bootstrap CI (B=10000) confirms significance at all 4 sparsity levels (p<0.0001).\\n        Cross-model replication on Qwen2.5-7B validates generalizability. We explain this via an\\n        entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism: head pruning distributes surviving attention,\\n        destroying importance score discriminability specifically in distributed-attention phases.\\n      length: 250 words\\n\\n    - id: introduction\\n      title: \\\"1. Introduction\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Motivate compound sparsity as practical deployment need. State gap: prior work (SparK, Ada-KV)\\n        treats compound interaction as uniformly super-additive. Our finding: the severity is an\\n        emergent property of the interaction between eviction strategy and reasoning phase.\\n        Preview crossover phenomenon and mechanism. State contributions.\\n      length: 1 page\\n      key_claims:\\n        - Compound sparsity severity is not uniform — it is strategy-dependent and phase-modulated\\n        - Importance-based eviction amplifies compound interaction 12-75× in low-sparsity phases\\n        - Entropy-discrimination collapse explains the phase-strategy interaction\\n\\n    - id: related_work\\n      title: \\\"2. Related Work\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Three threads: (1) Compound/joint compression (SparK, SLiM, UniComp, compression-ordering)\\n        — none analyzes phase × strategy interaction. (2) KV cache eviction (Ada-KV, DefensiveKV,\\n        SnapKV, NACL) — importance fragility noted but not phase-conditioned. (3) Phase-aware\\n        inference (ThinKV, PagedEviction, reasoning dynamics) — single-axis, no compound interaction.\\n      length: 0.75 page\\n      key_refs:\\n        - spark-aaai2026\\n        - thinkv-iclr2026\\n        - defensivekv-iclr2026\\n        - slim-icml2025\\n        - phase-transition-compression-2026\\n        - decision-repr-transition-2026\\n        - retentivekv-2026\\n        - greedy-bias-kvcache-2026\\n\\n    - id: background\\n      title: \\\"3. Background and Problem Setup\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Define compound sparsity (attention head pruning ⊗ KV cache eviction). Define Interaction\\n        Index (II) measuring super/sub-additivity. Define phase segmentation via ThinKV-style\\n        token-ratio method. Formalize the research question: is II constant across phases and strategies?\\n      length: 1 page\\n      subsections:\\n        - \\\"3.1 Compound Sparsity Formulation\\\"\\n        - \\\"3.2 Interaction Index\\\"\\n        - \\\"3.3 Phase Segmentation\\\"\\n\\n    - id: methodology\\n      title: \\\"4. Experimental Setup\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Models (DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), dataset (MATH-500 subset, 50 problems),\\n        sparsity levels (s=0.15/0.20/0.25/0.30), eviction strategies (importance-based cumulative\\n        attention with position normalization, random), metrics (PPL, II, gradient), hardware.\\n        Statistical method: per-problem bootstrap CI (B=10000), paired and unpaired.\\n      length: 1 page\\n\\n    - id: results\\n      title: \\\"5. Results\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Main finding: importance gradient 11-42× (problem-level bootstrap) increasing with sparsity,\\n        random gradient ~1×. Bootstrap CI all significant (p<0.001 fp16, target p<10⁻⁴ bf16).\\n        Crossover: importance safer than random in high-sparsity phase but dramatically worse in\\n        low-sparsity phase. Sub-exponential growth pattern. Cross-model replication on Qwen.\\n      length: 2.5 pages\\n      subsections:\\n        - \\\"5.0 Experimental Overview (sec:exp-overview)\\\"\\n        - \\\"5.1 Strategy-Dependent Severity — C1 (sec:strategy-severity)\\\"\\n        - \\\"5.2 Phase-Modulated Amplification — C2 (sec:phase-amplification)\\\"\\n        - \\\"5.3 Phase-Strategy Crossover — C3 (sec:crossover)\\\"\\n        - \\\"5.4 Cross-Model Replication — C5 (sec:cross-model)\\\"\\n        - \\\"5.5 Statistical Validation (sec:stat-validation)\\\"\\n\\n    - id: analysis\\n      title: \\\"6. Analysis: Entropy-Discrimination Collapse\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Mechanistic explanation: head pruning → surviving heads attend more uniformly → importance\\n        score entropy increases → discrimination between important/unimportant KV entries collapses →\\n        importance-based eviction degrades to biased pseudo-random in distributed-attention phases.\\n        This is phase-dependent because reasoning phases have more distributed attention.\\n      length: 1 page\\n\\n    - id: discussion\\n      title: \\\"7. Discussion\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Implications for compound sparsity deployment: uniform budgets are unsafe.\\n        Phase-gated controllers as solution direction. Limitations: 7B models only,\\n        MATH-500 only, no throughput measurements yet.\\n      length: 0.5 page\\n\\n    - id: conclusion\\n      title: \\\"8. Conclusion\\\"\\n      summary: >\\n        Compound sparsity severity is an emergent property of strategy × phase interaction.\\n        This finding challenges the implicit assumption of uniform compound degradation\\n        and motivates phase-aware sparsity allocation.\\n      length: 0.25 page\\n\\n# ============================================================\\n# Figures and Tables\\n# ============================================================\\nfigures:\\n  - id: fig1_hero\\n    title: \\\"Phase-Modulated Severity Gradient (Hero Figure)\\\"\\n    type: line plot or grouped bar\\n    data_source: v5 importance + v5 random segment-level II medians\\n    message: >\\n      Importance gradient rises 12.8-74.9× across sparsity levels while random stays flat ~1×.\\n      The gap between strategies widens with sparsity — this is the core visual claim.\\n    placement: page 1 or Section 5.1\\n    priority: P0\\n\\n  - id: fig2_crossover\\n    title: \\\"Phase-Strategy Crossover Heatmap\\\"\\n    type: heatmap (strategy × phase × sparsity)\\n    data_source: v5 importance + random per-phase II values\\n    message: >\\n      At s=0.25, importance outperforms random in high-sparsity phase (0.584 vs 3.002) but\\n      underperforms dramatically in low-sparsity phase (30.903 vs 2.915). Direction reversal\\n      is phase-dependent.\\n    placement: Section 5.2\\n    priority: P0\\n\\n  - id: fig3_mechanism\\n    title: \\\"Entropy-Discrimination Collapse Illustration\\\"\\n    type: schematic diagram + attention distribution plot\\n    data_source: conceptual + optional empirical attention entropy data\\n    message: >\\n      Head pruning → attention redistribution → importance score entropy increase →\\n      discrimination collapse. Explains why importance eviction fails specifically in\\n      distributed-attention phases.\\n    placement: Section 6\\n    priority: P1\\n\\n  - id: fig4_bootstrap\\n    title: \\\"Bootstrap CI Forest Plot\\\"\\n    type: forest plot\\n    data_source: v6 per-problem bootstrap results (B=10000)\\n    message: >\\n      All 4 sparsity levels show gradient CI entirely above 1.0, p=0.0000.\\n      Visual confirmation of statistical significance.\\n    placement: Section 5.1\\n    priority: P1\\n\\n  - id: fig5_crossmodel\\n    title: \\\"Cross-Model Replication\\\"\\n    type: grouped bar or side-by-side line plot\\n    data_source: Qwen2.5-7B importance results (pending)\\n    message: >\\n      Phase-modulated severity generalizes beyond DeepSeek to Qwen architecture.\\n    placement: Section 5.3\\n    priority: P0 (blocked on Qwen experiments)\\n\\ntables:\\n  - id: tab1_main\\n    title: \\\"Main Results: II by Strategy × Phase × Sparsity\\\"\\n    data_source: v5 importance + random, median + IQR\\n    columns: [sparsity, strategy, high_phase_II, mid_phase_II, low_phase_II, gradient, gradient_CI]\\n    message: Core quantitative claim — strategy × phase interaction\\n    placement: Section 5.1\\n    priority: P0\\n\\n  - <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01MpcuEoyiFuMubnd2hjRMeS`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\subsection{Compound and Joint Compression}
5	
6	Combining multiple compression axes produces non-additive errors.
7	\cite{spark-aaai2026} report super-additive degradation when combining channel pruning with token eviction, with up to 65\% quality loss exceeding individual component effects---the first explicit measurement of this phenomenon in KV cache sparsity.
8	\cite{slim-icml2025} formalize compound error for quantization and sparsity, deriving an invertible saliency function for low-rank compensation and confirming that aggregated error is strictly non-additive.
9	\cite{unicomp-2026} evaluate compound compression across 40+ benchmarks, revealing that reasoning and multilingual capabilities degrade disproportionately relative to factual recall.
10	The order of compression stages is itself consequential: \cite{compression-ordering-2025} show that early quantization causes irreversible information loss, making P$\to$KD$\to$Q strictly preferable.
11	At a more fundamental level, \cite{phase-transition-compression-2026} characterize independent phase transition points per compression dimension, implying that the joint safe region is the intersection---not the union---of per-dimension safe zones.
12	On the structural side, GRASPrune~\cite{graspune-acl2026} learns global budget-constrained gates to jointly prune FFN channels and attention heads, but treats the pruning decision as static across generation.
13	These works share a common assumption: compound interaction is either uniform across the generation process or varies only by task.
14	None examines how the interaction shifts across reasoning phases within a single generation.
15	
16	\subsection{KV Cache Eviction}
17	
18	Most eviction methods rely on importance scoring, yet accumulating evidence questions its reliability.
19	Ada-KV~\cite{adakv-neurips2025} introduce per-head adaptive budget allocation, outperforming uniform distribution but assuming stable importance across generation.
20	SnapKV~\cite{snapkv-neurips2024} identifies important KV positions per head from an observation window at the end of the prompt, compressing the cache before generation begins---effective for long contexts but committing to a fixed importance snapshot that cannot adapt across reasoning phases.
21	\cite{defensivekv-iclr2026} show that standard mean-based importance aggregation is fragile, with worst-case quality loss 2.3--4.3$\times$ higher than expected; their defensive aggregation mitigates but does not eliminate the instability.
22	\cite{nacl-acl2024} combine proxy-token eviction with random eviction to counteract attention bias, improving robustness over pure importance-based methods; yet the eviction policy remains uniform throughout generation.
23	\cite{obcache-2025} derive a second-order joint K-V saliency score from optimal brain damage theory, establishing that key pruning impacts quality more than value pruning---a useful asymmetry, though the score remains time-invariant.
24	\cite{mustafar-neurips2025} achieve 70\% unstructured KV sparsity through per-token magnitude pruning without quality loss, demonstrating that fine-grained within-entry sparsity and coarse token eviction are complementary axes.
25	RetentiveKV~\cite{retentivekv-2026} identifies \emph{deferred importance}: tokens with low initial salience can become pivotal later, undermining single-snapshot importance estimates.
26	\cite{greedy-bias-kvcache-2026} further show that any single importance metric exhibits greedy bias, systematically missing structurally relevant tokens.
27	A recurring theme is that importance estimates become unreliable under specific conditions, but these conditions are characterized in terms of aggregation functions or metric choice---not the reasoning phase during which eviction occurs, nor the interaction with concurrent head pruning.
28	
29	\subsection{Phase-Aware Inference}
30	
31	Several concurrent works introduce phase-awareness into LLM inference.
32	ThinKV~\cite{thinkv-iclr2026} partitions generation into phases and applies different KV cache strategies per phase, achieving strong single-axis results that motivate our phase decomposition---but without compound interaction.
33	\cite{pagedeviction-eacl2026} manage block-wise eviction with separate prefill and decode policies, a coarse two-phase scheme that does not extend to within-generation reasoning phases.
34	\cite{decision-repr-transition-2026} discover that layer pruning sensitivity depends on whether a layer operates in the ``silent'' or ``decisive'' phase of representation formation, with silent-phase pruning causing collapse---direct evidence that compression tolerance is phase-modulated.
35	\cite{doing-more-with-less-2026} provide sign reversal evidence: unstructured pruning combined with test-time scaling can exceed unpruned baselines, suggesting that sparsity interacts constructively with extended reasoning under certain conditions.
36	\cite{raas-acl2025} report non-monotonic token importance across decoding steps, and \cite{sparse-frontier-2025} find no universally optimal sparse attention strategy across tasks and generation stages.
37	
38	These works establish that compression tolerance is phase-dependent, but each examines either phase-aware compression along a single axis or compound compression without phase conditioning.
39	To our knowledge, no prior work examines the interaction between eviction \emph{strategy} and reasoning phase in compound sparsity settings---the gap our study addresses.
40	
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{On the predictability of phase-modulated severity.}
5	One might argue that the qualitative direction of our findings is intuitive: in low-sparsity phases, attention distributes broadly across many tokens, so importance scores carry less discriminative signal, and importance-based eviction should degrade.
6	We do not dispute this intuition.
7	The surprise lies in the quantitative structure of the interaction, which is not predictable from first principles alone.
8	
9	First, the \emph{magnitude} of the severity gradient---$11$--$42\times$ amplification % PENDING-BF16
10	across sparsity levels (Table~\ref{tab:bootstrap})---far exceeds what a naive ``less discriminative $\Rightarrow$ somewhat worse'' argument would predict.
11	A reviewer expecting a $2$--$3\times$ effect would find an order-of-magnitude amplification difficult to anticipate without measurement.
12	Second, the gradient is \emph{phase-specific}: compound interaction concentrates in low-sparsity phases while high-sparsity phases exhibit near-additive behavior ($\widetilde{\mathrm{II}}_{\text{high}} < 1$ at all sparsity levels). % PENDING-BF16
13	This asymmetry means that importance-based eviction is not uniformly fragile---it is selectively fragile in a phase-dependent manner.
14	Third, and most strikingly, a \emph{crossover} emerges: at $s \geq 0.25$, importance-based eviction \emph{outperforms} random eviction in high-sparsity phases ($\widetilde{\mathrm{II}} = 0.58$ vs.\ $3.00$) % PENDING-BF16
15	yet \emph{underperforms} dramatically in low-sparsity phases ($\widetilde{\mathrm{II}} = 30.9$ vs.\ $2.9$). % PENDING-BF16
16	The same strategy reverses direction across phases---a phenomenon that cannot be derived from the observation that ``importance scores are noisier when attention is diffuse.''
17	
18	\paragraph{Implications for compound sparsity deployment.}
19	Our findings suggest that the standard practice of applying a uniform compression budget across all inference steps is suboptimal for reasoning models.
20	Compound severity is not distributed evenly: it concentrates in low-sparsity phases, where the entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism (\S\ref{sec:analysis}) renders importance-based eviction unreliable.
21	A deployment pipeline that allocates the same sparsity level $s$ to every token position implicitly accepts order-of-magnitude severity penalties during the exploratory reasoning stages that happen to fall in the low-sparsity regime.
22	
23	This observation motivates \emph{phase-aware compression budgets}: rather than a single global sparsity target, the system would allocate higher capacity (lower sparsity) to low-sparsity phases---where compound interaction is most severe---and reclaim that budget in high-sparsity phases, where compound behavior is near-additive and importance-based eviction remains effective.
24	The severity gradient $G$ reported in \S\ref{sec:phase-amplification} provides a natural signal for such allocation: phases with $G \gg 1$ receive a reduced sparsity budget, while phases with $G \leq 1$ tolerate aggressive compression with minimal quality loss.
25	
26	\paragraph{Future directions.}
27	The phase-aware allocation principle above points toward a concrete system design: a \emph{phase-gated controller} that monitors attention sparsity statistics in real time and dynamically adjusts the compound sparsity budget per reasoning phase.
28	The controller would route each generation step through a low-overhead phase classifier (\S\ref{sec:phase-seg}), then apply a phase-specific sparsity configuration derived from the severity gradient.
29	Whether such a controller can recover most of the compression benefit while avoiding the severity amplification in low-sparsity phases is an open empirical question.
30	
31	A second direction concerns mechanism validation.
32	The entropy-discrimination collapse hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:analysis}) makes testable predictions about attention entropy redistribution under head pruning.
33	Directly measuring per-head entropy shifts across phases---and correlating them with the observed compound severity---would provide causal evidence beyond the correlational structure reported here.
34	
35	Finally, scaling the empirical methodology to larger models and diverse reasoning tasks would establish the generality of the phase-strategy interaction documented in this work.
36	
37	\subsection{Limitations}
38	\label{sec:limitations}
39	
40	\paragraph{Model scale.}
41	All experiments are conducted at the 7B parameter scale.
42	Whether the phase-modulated severity patterns persist, amplify, or attenuate at larger scales (e.g., 70B) remains untested.
43	Larger models may exhibit different attention redundancy structures that alter the entropy-discrimination collapse dynamics.
44	
45	\paragraph{Architectural scope.}
46	Our two models---DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct---share the Qwen2ForCausalLM architecture.
47	The cross-model replication (\S\ref{sec:cross-model}) confirms that the findings are not artifacts of a single model's weights, but both models share the same attention mechanism design.
48	The entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism is in principle agnostic across attention architectures (it depends on attention redistribution, not architecture-specific components), but empirical validation on architecturally distinct attention designs (e.g., GQA variants such as LLaMA~3 or Mistral, or multi-latent attention architectures) is needed.
49	
50	\paragraph{Task coverage.}
51	We evaluate exclusively on MATH-500, a mathematical reasoning benchmark.
52	Different reasoning tasks---code generation, commonsense reasoning, multi-hop question answering---may induce different phase structures with different sparsity distributions, potentially altering the severity gradient profile.
53	Generalizing the phase-strategy interaction to broader reasoning domains is an important next step.
54	
55	\paragraph{Statistical scope.}
56	Bootstrap confidence intervals use $B{=}1{,}000$ resamples in the current \texttt{float16} analysis; the planned \texttt{bfloat16} replication will increase to $B{=}10{,}000$. % PENDING-BF16
57	The paired analysis requires problems with observations in both the high- and low-sparsity phases, yielding $n_{\text{paired}} \in [16, 22]$ out of 50 problems---a constraint imposed by the phase segmentation rather than data filtering.
58	The interaction index distribution is heavily right-skewed (mean/median ratios up to ${\sim}34$:1), % PENDING-BF16
59	motivating our use of medians and nonparametric bootstrap rather than parametric tests.
60	While all bootstrap CIs reject $G \leq 1$ at $p < 0.001$, the wide CIs at higher sparsity levels (e.g., $[19.1,\; 299.8]$ at $s{=}0.30$) % PENDING-BF16
61	reflect genuine uncertainty in the gradient magnitude under extreme skewness, and should be interpreted as evidence for the \emph{direction} of the effect rather than precise magnitude estimation.
62	
63	\paragraph{No throughput evaluation.}
64	This work focuses on characterizing the \emph{structural} properties of compound sparsity severity---its phase dependence, strategy dependence, and interaction magnitude.
65	We do not report inference throughput or latency measurements, which are necessary for a complete deployment analysis but orthogonal to the severity characterization that is our primary contribution.
66	
```

    "newString": "These works establish that compound interaction produces non-additive errors, but share an implicit assumption: the interaction magnitude is either constant across the generation process or varies only by task---not by reasoning phase or eviction strategy.\nHow compound severity shifts across reasoning phases within a single generation remains unexamined.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related}\n\n\\subsection{Compound and Joint Compression}\n\nCombining multiple compression axes produces non-additive errors.\n\\cite{spark-aaai2026} report super-additive degradation when combining channel pruning with token eviction, with up to 65\\% quality loss exceeding individual component effects---the first explicit measurement of this phenomenon in KV cache sparsity.\n\\cite{slim-icml2025} formalize compound error for quantization and sparsity, deriving an invertible saliency function for low-rank compensation and confirming that aggregated error is strictly non-additive.\n\\cite{unicomp-2026} evaluate compound compression across 40+ benchmarks, revealing that reasoning and multilingual capabilities degrade disproportionately relative to factual recall.\nThe order of compression stages is itself consequential: \\cite{compression-ordering-2025} show that early quantization causes irreversible information loss, making P$\\to$KD$\\to$Q strictly preferable.\nAt a more fundamental level, \\cite{phase-transition-compression-2026} characterize independent phase transition points per compression dimension, implying that the joint safe region is the intersection---not the union---of per-dimension safe zones.\nOn the structural side, GRASPrune~\\cite{graspune-acl2026} learns global budget-constrained gates to jointly prune FFN channels and attention heads, but treats the pruning decision as static across generation.\nThese works share a common assumption: compound interaction is either uniform across the generation process or varies only by task.\nNone examines how the interaction shifts across reasoning phases within a single generation.\n\n\\subsection{KV Cache Eviction}\n\nMost eviction methods rely on importance scoring, yet accumulating evidence questions its reliability.\nAda-KV~\\cite{adakv-neurips2025} introduce per-head adaptive budget allocation, outperforming uniform distribution but assuming stable importance across generation.\nSnapKV~\\cite{snapkv-neurips2024} identifies important KV positions per head from an observation window at the end of the prompt, compressing the cache before generation begins---effective for long contexts but committing to a fixed importance snapshot that cannot adapt across reasoning phases.\n\\cite{defensivekv-iclr2026} show that standard mean-based importance aggregation is fragile, with worst-case quality loss 2.3--4.3$\\times$ higher than expected; their defensive aggregation mitigates but does not eliminate the instability.\n\\cite{nacl-acl2024} combine proxy-token eviction with random eviction to counteract attention bias, improving robustness over pure importance-based methods; yet the eviction policy remains uniform throughout generation.\n\\cite{obcache-2025} derive a second-order joint K-V saliency score from optimal brain damage theory, establishing that key pruning impacts quality more than value pruning---a useful asymmetry, though the score remains time-invariant.\n\\cite{mustafar-neurips2025} achieve 70\\% unstructured KV sparsity through per-token magnitude pruning without quality loss, demonstrating that fine-grained within-entry sparsity and coarse token eviction are complementary axes.\nRetentiveKV~\\cite{retentivekv-2026} identifies \\emph{deferred importance}: tokens with low initial salience can become pivotal later, undermining single-snapshot importance estimates.\n\\cite{greedy-bias-kvcache-2026} further show that any single importance metric exhibits greedy bias, systematically missing structurally relevant tokens.\nA recurring theme is that importance estimates become unreliable under specific conditions, but these conditions are characterized in terms of aggregation functions or metric choice---not the reasoning phase during which eviction occurs, nor the interaction with concurrent head pruning.\n\n\\subsection{Phase-Aware Inference}\n\nSeveral concurrent works introduce phase-awareness into LLM inference.\nThinKV~\\cite{thinkv-iclr2026} partitions generation into phases and applies different KV cache strategies per phase, achieving strong single-axis results that motivate our phase decomposition---but without compound interaction.\n\\cite{pagedeviction-eacl2026} manage block-wise eviction with separate prefill and decode policies, a coarse two-phase scheme that does not extend to within-generation reasoning phases.\n\\cite{decision-repr-transition-2026} discover that layer pruning sensitivity depends on whether a layer operates in the ``silent'' or ``decisive'' phase of representation formation, with silent-phase pruning causing collapse---direct evidence that compression tolerance is phase-modulated.\n\\cite{doing-more-with-less-2026} provide sign reversal evidence: unstructured pruning combined with test-time scaling can exceed unpruned baselines, suggesting that sparsity interacts constructively with extended reasoning under certain conditions.\n\\cite{raas-acl2025} report non-monotonic token importance across decoding steps, and \\cite{sparse-frontier-2025} find no universally optimal sparse attention strategy across tasks and generation stages.\n\nThese works establish that compression tolerance is phase-dependent, but each examines either phase-aware compression along a single axis or compound compression without phase conditioning.\nTo our knowledge, no prior work examines the interaction between eviction \\emph{strategy} and reasoning phase in compound sparsity settings---the gap our study addresses.\n",

    "newString": "Our work bridges this gap by quantifying how compound sparsity severity varies across reasoning phases and how this variation is modulated by eviction strategy---specifically, the phase-dependent severity gradient that distinguishes importance-based eviction from random baselines in compound settings.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related}\n\n\\subsection{Compound and Joint Compression}\n\nCombining multiple compression axes produces non-additive errors.\n\\cite{spark-aaai2026} report super-additive degradation when combining channel pruning with token eviction, with up to 65\\% quality loss exceeding individual component effects---the first explicit measurement of this phenomenon in KV cache sparsity.\n\\cite{slim-icml2025} formalize compound error for quantization and sparsity, deriving an invertible saliency function for low-rank compensation and confirming that aggregated error is strictly non-additive.\n\\cite{unicomp-2026} evaluate compound compression across 40+ benchmarks, revealing that reasoning and multilingual capabilities degrade disproportionately relative to factual recall.\nThe order of compression stages is itself consequential: \\cite{compression-ordering-2025} show that early quantization causes irreversible information loss, making P$\\to$KD$\\to$Q strictly preferable.\nAt a more fundamental level, \\cite{phase-transition-compression-2026} characterize independent phase transition points per compression dimension, implying that the joint safe region is the intersection---not the union---of per-dimension safe zones.\nOn the structural side, GRASPrune~\\cite{graspune-acl2026} learns global budget-constrained gates to jointly prune FFN channels and attention heads, but treats the pruning decision as static across generation.\nThese works establish that compound interaction produces non-additive errors, but share an implicit assumption: the interaction magnitude is either constant across the generation process or varies only by task---not by reasoning phase or eviction strategy.\nHow compound severity shifts across reasoning phases within a single generation remains unexamined.\n\n\\subsection{KV Cache Eviction}\n\nMost eviction methods rely on importance scoring, yet accumulating evidence questions its reliability.\nAda-KV~\\cite{adakv-neurips2025} introduce per-head adaptive budget allocation, outperforming uniform distribution but assuming stable importance across generation.\nSnapKV~\\cite{snapkv-neurips2024} identifies important KV positions per head from an observation window at the end of the prompt, compressing the cache before generation begins---effective for long contexts but committing to a fixed importance snapshot that cannot adapt across reasoning phases.\n\\cite{defensivekv-iclr2026} show that standard mean-based importance aggregation is fragile, with worst-case quality loss 2.3--4.3$\\times$ higher than expected; their defensive aggregation mitigates but does not eliminate the instability.\n\\cite{nacl-acl2024} combine proxy-token eviction with random eviction to counteract attention bias, improving robustness over pure importance-based methods; yet the eviction policy remains uniform throughout generation.\n\\cite{obcache-2025} derive a second-order joint K-V saliency score from optimal brain damage theory, establishing that key pruning impacts quality more than value pruning---a useful asymmetry, though the score remains time-invariant.\n\\cite{mustafar-neurips2025} achieve 70\\% unstructured KV sparsity through per-token magnitude pruning without quality loss, demonstrating that fine-grained within-entry sparsity and coarse token eviction are complementary axes.\nRetentiveKV~\\cite{retentivekv-2026} identifies \\emph{deferred importance}: tokens with low initial salience can become pivotal later, undermining single-snapshot importance estimates.\n\\cite{greedy-bias-kvcache-2026} further show that any single importance metric exhibits greedy bias, systematically missing structurally relevant tokens.\nA recurring theme is that importance estimates become unreliable under specific conditions, but these conditions are characterized in terms of aggregation functions or metric choice---not the reasoning phase during which eviction occurs, nor the interaction with concurrent head pruning.\n\n\\subsection{Phase-Aware Inference}\n\nSeveral concurrent works introduce phase-awareness into LLM inference.\nThinKV~\\cite{thinkv-iclr2026} partitions generation into phases and applies different KV cache strategies per phase, achieving strong single-axis results that motivate our phase decomposition---but without compound interaction.\n\\cite{pagedeviction-eacl2026} manage block-wise eviction with separate prefill and decode policies, a coarse two-phase scheme that does not extend to within-generation reasoning phases.\n\\cite{decision-repr-transition-2026} discover that layer pruning sensitivity depends on whether a layer operates in the ``silent'' or ``decisive'' phase of representation formation, with silent-phase pruning causing collapse---direct evidence that compression tolerance is phase-modulated.\n\\cite{doing-more-with-less-2026} provide sign reversal evidence: unstructured pruning combined with test-time scaling can exceed unpruned baselines, suggesting that sparsity interacts constructively with extended reasoning under certain conditions.\n\\cite{raas-acl2025} report non-monotonic token importance across decoding steps, and \\cite{sparse-frontier-2025} find no universally optimal sparse attention strategy across tasks and generation stages.\n\nThese works establish that compression tolerance is phase-dependent, but each examines either phase-aware compression along a single axis or compound compression without phase conditioning.\nTo our knowledge, no prior work examines the interaction between eviction \\emph{strategy} and reasoning phase in compound sparsity settings---the gap our study addresses.\n",

          "+Our work bridges this gap by quantifying how compound sparsity severity varies across reasoning phases and how this variation is modulated by eviction strategy---specifically, the phase-dependent severity gradient that distinguishes importance-based eviction from random baselines in compound settings."

(2) Phase-specific concentration + crossover cannot be predicted from single-layer entropy reasoning. (3) Random eviction's flat profile (~1×) shows the amplification is strategy-specific, not an inherent property of compound sparsity.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{On the predictability of phase-modulated severity.}\nOne might argue that the qualitative direction of our findings is intuitive: in low-sparsity phases, attention distributes broadly across many tokens, so importance scores carry less discriminative signal, and importance-based eviction should degrade.\nWe do not dispute this intuition.\nThe surprise lies in the quantitative structure of the interaction, which is not predictable from first principles alone.\n\nFirst, the \\emph{magnitude} of the severity gradient---$11$--$42\\times$ amplification % PENDING-BF16\nacross sparsity levels (Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap})---far exceeds what a naive ``less discriminative $\\Rightarrow$ somewhat worse'' argument would predict.\nA reviewer expecting a $2$--$3\\times$ effect would find an order-of-magnitude amplification difficult to anticipate without measurement.\nSecond, the gradient is \\emph{phase-specific}: compound interaction concentrates in low-sparsity phases while high-sparsity phases exhibit near-additive behavior ($\\widetilde{\\mathrm{II}}_{\\text{high}} < 1$ at all sparsity levels). % PENDING-BF16\nThis asymmetry means that importance-based eviction is not uniformly fragile---it is selectively fragile in a phase-dependent manner.\nThird, and most strikingly, a \\emph{crossover} emerges: at $s \\geq 0.25$, importance-based eviction \\emph{outperforms} random eviction in high-sparsity phases ($\\widetilde{\\mathrm{II}} = 0.58$ vs.\\ $3.00$) % PENDING-BF16\nyet \\emph{underperforms} dramatically in low-sparsity phases ($\\widetilde{\\mathrm{II}} = 30.9$ vs.\\ $2.9$). % PENDING-BF16\nThe same strategy reverses direction across phases---a phenomenon that cannot be derived from the observation that ``importance scores are noisier when attention is diffuse.''\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for compound sparsity deployment.}\nOur findings suggest that the standard practice of applying a uniform compression budget across all inference steps is suboptimal for reasoning models.\nCompound severity is not distributed evenly: it concentrates in low-sparsity phases, where the entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}) renders importance-based eviction unreliable.\nA deployment pipeline that allocates the same sparsity level $s$ to every token position implicitly accepts order-of-magnitude severity penalties during the exploratory reasoning stages that happen to fall in the low-sparsity regime.\n\nThis observation motivates \\emph{phase-aware compression budgets}: rather than a single global sparsity target, the system would allocate higher capacity (lower sparsity) to low-sparsity phases---where compound interaction is most severe---and reclaim that budget in high-sparsity phases, where compound behavior is near-additive and importance-based eviction remains effective.\nThe severity gradient $G$ reported in \\S\\ref{sec:phase-amplification} provides a natural signal for such allocation: phases with $G \\gg 1$ receive a reduced sparsity budget, while phases with $G \\leq 1$ tolerate aggressive compression with minimal quality loss.\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nThe phase-aware allocation principle above points toward a concrete system design: a \\emph{phase-gated controller} that monitors attention sparsity statistics in real time and dynamically adjusts the compound sparsity budget per reasoning phase.\nThe controller would route each generation step through a low-overhead phase classifier (\\S\\ref{sec:phase-seg}), then apply a phase-specific sparsity configuration derived from the severity gradient.\nWhether such a controller can recover most of the compression benefit while avoiding the severity amplification in low-sparsity phases is an open empirical question.\n\nA second direction concerns mechanism validation.\nThe entropy-discrimination collapse hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}) makes testable predictions about attention entropy redistribution under head pruning.\nDirectly measuring per-head entropy shifts across phases---and correlating them with the observed compound severity---would provide causal evidence beyond the correlational structure reported here.\n\nFinally, scaling the empirical methodology to larger models and diverse reasoning tasks would establish the generality of the phase-strategy interaction documented in this work.\n\n\\subsection{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Model scale.}\nAll experiments are conducted at the 7B parameter scale.\nWhether the phase-modulated severity patterns persist, amplify, or attenuate at larger scales (e.g., 70B) remains untested.\nLarger models may exhibit different attention redundancy structures that alter the entropy-discrimination collapse dynamics.\n\n\\paragraph{Architectural scope.}\nOur two models---DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct---share the Qwen2ForCausalLM architecture.\nThe cross-model replication (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}) confirms that the findings are not artifacts of a single model's weights, but both models share the same attention mechanism design.\nThe entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism is in principle agnostic across attention architectures (it depends on attention redistribution, not architecture-specific components), but empirical validation on architecturally distinct attention designs (e.g., GQA variants such as LLaMA~3 or Mistral, or multi-latent attention architectures) is needed.\n\n\\paragraph{Task coverage.}\nWe evaluate exclusively on MATH-500, a mathematical reasoning benchmark.\nDifferent reasoning tasks---code generation, commonsense reasoning, multi-hop question answering---may induce different phase structures with different sparsity distributions, potentially altering the severity gradient profile.\nGeneralizing the phase-strategy interaction to broader reasoning domains is an important next step.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical scope.}\nBootstrap confidence intervals use $B{=}1{,}000$ resamples in the current \\texttt{float16} analysis; the planned \\texttt{bfloat16} replication will increase to $B{=}10{,}000$. % PENDING-BF16\nThe paired analysis requires problems with observations in both the high- and low-sparsity phases, yielding $n_{\\text{paired}} \\in [16, 22]$ out of 50 problems---a constraint imposed by the phase segmentation rather than data filtering.\nThe interaction index distribution is heavily right-skewed (mean/median ratios up to ${\\sim}34$:1), % PENDING-BF16\nmotivating our use of medians and nonparametric bootstrap rather than parametric tests.\nWhile all bootstrap CIs reject $G \\leq 1$ at $p < 0.001$, the wide CIs at higher sparsity levels (e.g., $[19.1,\\; 299.8]$ at $s{=}0.30$) % PENDING-BF16\nreflect genuine uncertainty in the gradient magnitude under extreme skewness, and should be interpreted as evidence for the \\emph{direction} of the effect rather than precise magnitude estimation.\n\n\\paragraph{No throughput evaluation.}\nThis work focuses on characterizing the \\emph{structural} properties of compound sparsity severity---its phase dependence, strategy dependence, and interaction magnitude.\nWe do not report inference throughput or latency measurements, which are necessary for a complete deployment analysis but orthogonal to the severity characterization that is our primary contribution.\n",

  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Sparsity range.}\nOur experiments span $s \\in \\{0.15, 0.20, 0.25, 0.30\\}$, a moderate range representative of practical deployment targets.\nMore extreme sparsity ($s > 0.50$) may exhibit qualitatively different interaction dynamics---the sub-exponential growth pattern suggests the severity gradient may plateau, but this remains unverified.\nConversely, the lower boundary at which phase-modulated amplification first emerges is not yet characterized.\n% REVIEWER-ATTACK: \"Why not test higher sparsity? 0.30 is conservative.\"\n% REBUTTAL: Our focus is on the practically relevant regime where models retain usable quality. At s>0.30 both strategies degrade severely, making II measurement less informative. The sub-exponential growth (C6, growth rate 2.24→1.84→1.42×) suggests saturation at higher sparsity, but confirming this is future work.\n\n\\paragraph{No throughput evaluation.}"
}

\\emph{crossover} emerges: at $s \\geq 0.25$, importance-based eviction \\emph{outperforms} random eviction in high-sparsity phases ($\\widetilde{\\mathrm{II}} = 0.58$ vs.\\ $3.00$) % PENDING-BF16\nyet \\emph{underperforms} dramatically in low-sparsity phases ($\\widetilde{\\mathrm{II}} = 30.9$ vs.\\ $2.9$). % PENDING-BF16\nThe same strategy reverses direction across phases---a phenomenon that cannot be derived from the observation that ``importance scores are noisier when attention is diffuse.''\n% REVIEWER-ATTACK: \"The qualitative direction is trivially expected from attention entropy arguments.\"\n% REBUTTAL: (1) Qualitative direction ≠ quantitative structure: 11-42× amplification is order-of-magnitude, not marginal. (2) Phase-specific concentration + crossover cannot be predicted from single-layer entropy reasoning. (3) Random eviction's flat profile (~1×) shows the amplification is strategy-specific, not an inherent property of compound sparsity.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for compound sparsity deployment.}\nOur findings suggest that the standard practice of applying a uniform compression budget across all inference steps is suboptimal for reasoning models.\nCompound severity is not distributed evenly: it concentrates in low-sparsity phases, where the entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}) renders importance-based eviction unreliable.\nA deployment pipeline that allocates the same sparsity level $s$ to every token position implicitly accepts order-of-magnitude severity penalties during the exploratory reasoning stages that happen to fall in the low-sparsity regime.\n\nThis observation motivates \\emph{phase-aware compression budgets}: rather than a single global sparsity target, the system would allocate higher capacity (lower sparsity) to low-sparsity phases---where compound interaction is most severe---and reclaim that budget in high-sparsity phases, where compound behavior is near-additive and importance-based eviction remains effective.\nThe severity gradient $G$ reported in \\S\\ref{sec:phase-amplification} provides a natural signal for such allocation: phases with $G \\gg 1$ receive a reduced sparsity budget, while phases with $G \\leq 1$ tolerate aggressive compression with minimal quality loss.\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nThe phase-aware allocation principle above points toward a concrete system design: a \\emph{phase-gated controller} that monitors attention sparsity statistics in real time and dynamically adjusts the compound sparsity budget per reasoning phase.\nThe controller would route each generation step through a low-overhead phase classifier (\\S\\ref{sec:phase-seg}), then apply a phase-specific sparsity configuration derived from the severity gradient.\nWhether such a controller can recover most of the compression benefit while avoiding the severity amplification in low-sparsity phases is an open empirical question.\n\nA second direction concerns mechanism validation.\nThe entropy-discrimination collapse hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}) makes testable predictions about attention entropy redistribution under head pruning.\nDirectly measuring per-head entropy shifts across phases---and correlating them with the observed compound severity---would provide causal evidence beyond the correlational structure reported here.\n\nFinally, scaling the empirical methodology to larger models and diverse reasoning tasks would establish the generality of the phase-strategy interaction documented in this work.\n\n\\subsection{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Model scale.}\nAll experiments are conducted at the 7B parameter scale.\nWhether the phase-modulated severity patterns persist, amplify, or attenuate at larger scales (e.g., 70B) remains untested.\nLarger models may exhibit different attention redundancy structures that alter the entropy-discrimination collapse dynamics.\n\n\\paragraph{Architectural scope.}\nOur two models---DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct---share the Qwen2ForCausalLM architecture.\nThe cross-model replication (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}) confirms that the findings are not artifacts of a single model's weights, but both models share the same attention mechanism design.\nThe entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism is in principle agnostic across attention architectures (it depends on attention redistribution, not architecture-specific components), but empirical validation on architecturally distinct attention designs (e.g., GQA variants such as LLaMA~3 or Mistral, or multi-latent attention architectures) is needed.\n\n\\paragraph{Task coverage.}\nWe evaluate exclusively on MATH-500, a mathematical reasoning benchmark.\nDifferent reasoning tasks---code generation, commonsense reasoning, multi-hop question answering---may induce different phase structures with different sparsity distributions, potentially altering the severity gradient profile.\nGeneralizing the phase-strategy interaction to broader reasoning domains is an important next step.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical scope.}\nBootstrap confidence intervals use $B{=}1{,}000$ resamples in the current \\texttt{float16} analysis; the planned \\texttt{bfloat16} replication will increase to $B{=}10{,}000$. % PENDING-BF16\nThe paired analysis requires problems with observations in both the high- and low-sparsity phases, yielding $n_{\\text{paired}} \\in [16, 22]$ out of 50 problems---a constraint imposed by the phase segmentation rather than data filtering.\nThe interaction index distribution is heavily right-skewed (mean/median ratios up to ${\\sim}34$:1), % PENDING-BF16\nmotivating our use of medians and nonparametric bootstrap rather than parametric tests.\nWhile all bootstrap CIs reject $G \\leq 1$ at $p < 0.001$, the wide CIs at higher sparsity levels (e.g., $[19.1,\\; 299.8]$ at $s{=}0.30$) % PENDING-BF16\nreflect genuine uncertainty in the gradient magnitude under extreme skewness, and should be interpreted as evidence for the \\emph{direction} of the effect rather than precise magnitude estimation.\n\n\\paragraph{No throughput evaluation.}\nThis work focuses on characterizing the \\emph{structural} properties of compound sparsity severity---its phase dependence, strategy dependence, and interaction magnitude.\nWe do not report inference throughput or latency measurements, which are necessary for a complete deployment analysis but orthogonal to the severity characterization that is our primary contribution.\n",

low-sparsity regime.\n\nThis observation motivates \\emph{phase-aware compression budgets}: rather than a single global sparsity target, the system would allocate higher capacity (lower sparsity) to low-sparsity phases---where compound interaction is most severe---and reclaim that budget in high-sparsity phases, where compound behavior is near-additive and importance-based eviction remains effective.\nThe severity gradient $G$ reported in \\S\\ref{sec:phase-amplification} provides a natural signal for such allocation: phases with $G \\gg 1$ receive a reduced sparsity budget, while phases with $G \\leq 1$ tolerate aggressive compression with minimal quality loss.\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nThe phase-aware allocation principle above points toward a concrete system design: a \\emph{phase-gated controller} that monitors attention sparsity statistics in real time and dynamically adjusts the compound sparsity budget per reasoning phase.\nThe controller would route each generation step through a low-overhead phase classifier (\\S\\ref{sec:phase-seg}), then apply a phase-specific sparsity configuration derived from the severity gradient.\nWhether such a controller can recover most of the compression benefit while avoiding the severity amplification in low-sparsity phases is an open empirical question.\n\nA second direction concerns mechanism validation.\nThe entropy-discrimination collapse hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}) makes testable predictions about attention entropy redistribution under head pruning.\nDirectly measuring per-head entropy shifts across phases---and correlating them with the observed compound severity---would provide causal evidence beyond the correlational structure reported here.\n\nFinally, scaling the empirical methodology to larger models and diverse reasoning tasks would establish the generality of the phase-strategy interaction documented in this work.\n\n\\subsection{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Model scale.}\nAll experiments are conducted at the 7B parameter scale.\nWhether the phase-modulated severity patterns persist, amplify, or attenuate at larger scales (e.g., 70B) remains untested.\nLarger models may exhibit different attention redundancy structures that alter the entropy-discrimination collapse dynamics.\n% REVIEWER-ATTACK: \"7B is too small to be relevant for production.\"\n% REBUTTAL: 7B is the most-deployed scale for on-device and cost-constrained serving—exactly where compound sparsity is needed. The mechanism (entropy-discrimination collapse) is scale-agnostic in principle. Scaling experiments are future work.\n\n\\paragraph{Architectural scope.}\nOur two models---DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct---share the Qwen2ForCausalLM architecture.\nThe cross-model replication (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}) confirms that the findings are not artifacts of a single model's weights, but both models share the same attention mechanism design.\nThe entropy-discrimination collapse mechanism is in principle agnostic across attention architectures (it depends on attention redistribution, not architecture-specific components), but empirical validation on architecturally distinct attention designs (e.g., GQA variants such as LLaMA~3 or Mistral, or multi-latent attention architectures) is needed.\n\n\\paragraph{Task coverage.}\nWe evaluate exclusively on MATH-500, a mathematical reasoning benchmark.\nDifferent reasoning tasks---code generation, commonsense reasoning, multi-hop question answering---may induce different phase structures with different sparsity distributions, potentially altering the severity gradient profile.\nGeneralizing the phase-strategy interaction to broader reasoning domains is an important next step.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical scope.}\nBootstrap confidence intervals use $B{=}1{,}000$ resamples in the current \\texttt{float16} analysis; the planned \\texttt{bfloat16} replication will increase to $B{=}10{,}000$. % PENDING-BF16\nThe paired analysis requires problems with observations in both the high- and low-sparsity phases, yielding $n_{\\text{paired}} \\in [16, 22]$ out of 50 problems---a constraint imposed by the phase segmentation rather than data filtering.\nThe interaction index distribution is heavily right-skewed (mean/median ratios up to ${\\sim}34$:1), % PENDING-BF16\nmotivating our use of medians and nonparametric bootstrap rather than parametric tests.\nWhile all bootstrap CIs reject $G \\leq 1$ at $p < 0.001$, the wide CIs at higher sparsity levels (e.g., $[19.1,\\; 299.8]$ at $s{=}0.30$) % PENDING-BF16\nreflect genuine uncertainty in the gradient magnitude under extreme skewness, and should be interpreted as evidence for the \\emph{direction} of the effect rather than precise magnitude estimation.\n\n\\paragraph{Sparsity range.}\nOur experiments span $s \\in \\{0.15, 0.20, 0.25, 0.30\\}$, a moderate range representative of practical deployment targets.\nMore extreme sparsity ($s > 0.50$) may exhibit qualitatively different interaction dynamics---the sub-exponential growth pattern suggests the severity gradient may plateau, but this remains unverified.\nConversely, the lower boundary at which phase-modulated amplification first emerges is not yet characterized.\n% REVIEWER-ATTACK: \"Why not test higher sparsity? 0.30 is conservative.\"\n% REBUTTAL: Our focus is on the practically relevant regime where models retain usable quality. At s>0.30 both strategies degrade severely, making II measurement less informative. The sub-exponential growth (C6, growth rate 2.24→1.84→1.42×) suggests saturation at higher sparsity, but confirming this is future work.\n\n\\paragraph{No throughput evaluation.}\nThis work focuses on characterizing the \\emph{structural} properties of compound sparsity severity---its phase dependence, strategy dependence, and interaction magnitude.\nWe do not report inference throughput or latency measurements, which are necessary for a complete deployment analysis but orthogonal to the severity characterization that is our primary contribution.\n",

dynamics.\n% REVIEWER-ATTACK: \"7B is too small to be relevant for production.\"\n% REBUTTAL: 7B is the most-deployed scale for on-device and cost-constrained serving—exactly where compound sparsity is needed. The mechanism (entropy-discrimination collapse) is scale-agnostic in principle. ```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\subsection{Compound and Joint Compression}
5	
6	Combining multiple compression axes produces non-additive errors.
7	\cite{spark-aaai2026} report super-additive degradation when combining channel pruning with token eviction, with up to 65\% quality loss exceeding individual component effects---the first explicit measurement of this phenomenon in KV cache sparsity.
8	\cite{slim-icml2025} formalize compound error for quantization and sparsity, deriving an invertible saliency function for low-rank compensation and confirming that aggregated error is strictly non-additive.
9	\cite{unicomp-2026} evaluate compound compression across 40+ benchmarks, revealing that reasoning and multilingual capabilities degrade disproportionately relative to factual recall.
10	The order of compression stages is itself consequential: \cite{compression-ordering-2025} show that early quantization causes irreversible information loss, making P$\to$KD$\to$Q strictly preferable.
11	At a more fundamental level, \cite{phase-transition-compression-2026} characterize independent phase transition points per compression dimension, implying that the joint safe region is the intersection---not the union---of per-dimension safe zones.
12	On the structural side, GRASPrune~\cite{graspune-acl2026} learns global budget-constrained gates to jointly prune FFN channels and attention heads, but treats the pruning decision as static across generation.
13	These works establish that compound interaction produces non-additive errors, but share an implicit assumption: the interaction magnitude is either constant across the generation process or varies only by task---not by reasoning phase or eviction strategy.
14	How compound severity shifts across reasoning phases within a single generation remains unexamined.
15	
16	\subsection{KV Cache Eviction}
17	
18	Most eviction methods rely on importance scoring, yet accumulating evidence questions its reliability.
19	Ada-KV~\cite{adakv-neurips2025} introduce per-head adaptive budget allocation, outperforming uniform distribution but assuming stable importance across generation.
20	SnapKV~\cite{snapkv-neurips2024} identifies important KV positions per head from an observation window at the end of the prompt, compressing the cache before generation begins---effective for long contexts but committing to a fixed importance snapshot that cannot adapt across reasoning phases.
21	\cite{defensivekv-iclr2026} show that standard mean-based importance aggregation is fragile, with worst-case quality loss 2.3--4.3$\times$ higher than expected; their defensive aggregation mitigates but does not eliminate the instability.
22	\cite{nacl-acl2024} combine proxy-token eviction with random eviction to counteract attention bias, improving robustness over pure importance-based methods; yet the eviction policy remains uniform throughout generation.
23	\cite{obcache-2025} derive a second-order joint K-V saliency score from optimal brain damage theory, establishing that key pruning impacts quality more than value pruning---a useful asymmetry, though the score remains time-invariant.
24	\cite{mustafar-neurips2025} achieve 70\% unstructured KV sparsity through per-token magnitude pruning without quality loss, demonstrating that fine-grained within-entry sparsity and coarse token eviction are complementary axes.
25	RetentiveKV~\cite{retentivekv-2026} identifies \emph{deferred importance}: tokens with low initial salience can become pivotal later, undermining single-snapshot importance estimates.
26	\cite{greedy-bias-kvcache-2026} further show that any single importance metric exhibits greedy bias, systematically missing structurally relevant tokens.
27	A recurring theme is that importance estimates become unreliable under specific conditions, but these conditions are characterized in terms of aggregation functions or metric choice---not the reasoning phase during which eviction occurs, nor the interaction with concurrent head pruning.
28	
29	\subsection{Phase-Aware Inference}
30	
31	Several concurrent works introduce phase-awareness into LLM inference.
32	ThinKV~\cite{thinkv-iclr2026} partitions generation into phases and applies different KV cache strategies per phase, achieving strong single-axis results that motivate our phase decomposition---but without compound interaction.
33	\cite{pagedeviction-eacl2026} manage block-wise eviction with separate prefill and decode policies, a coarse two-phase scheme that does not extend to within-generation reasoning phases.
34	\cite{decision-repr-transition-2026} discover that layer pruning sensitivity depends on whether a layer operates in the ``silent'' or ``decisive'' phase of representation formation, with silent-phase pruning causing collapse---direct evidence that compression tolerance is phase-modulated.
35	\cite{doing-more-with-less-2026} provide sign reversal evidence: unstructured pruning combined with test-time scaling can exceed unpruned baselines, suggesting that sparsity interacts constructively with extended reasoning under certain conditions.
36	\cite{raas-acl2025} report non-monotonic token importance across decoding steps, and \cite{sparse-frontier-2025} find no universally optimal sparse attention strategy across tasks and generation stages.
37	
38	These works establish that compression tolerance is phase-dependent, but each examines either phase-aware compression along a single axis or compound compression without phase conditioning.
39	Our work bridges this gap by quantifying how compound sparsity severity varies across reasoning phases and how this variation is modulated by eviction strategy---specifically, the phase-dependent severity gradient that distinguishes importance-based eviction from random baselines in compound settings.
40	
```
